美國股市：AI 成長主線未變，短線估值風險升溫
七月美股受伊朗戰爭不確定性、 聯準會態度轉鷹干擾 ，股市出現巨幅震盪 。 雖財報盈餘表現強勁，
但市場開始觀察資本支出擴張壓迫科技龍頭企業的現金流。指數上揚主要由微軟、蘋果、亞馬遜、輝
達、埃克森美孚、博通、摩根大通銀行、雪佛龍、萬事達卡、威士卡等企業帶動，表現相對弱勢的族
群包括美光、特斯拉、英特爾、應用材料、科林研發、科磊、晟碟、超微半導體、卡特彼勒、康寧等。
美國經濟整體仍維持穩健 ， 但第二季GDP 年化季增率由 2.1%放緩至 1.5%， 主要反映進口增加、
企業庫存調整及政府支出下降。民間消費仍是第二季經濟成長的主要支撐，年化季增率由第一季的

0.5%大幅回升至 3.2%，其中耐久財消費成長 6.8%、非耐久財成長 4.4%，服務消費亦由 0.5%回升
至 2.2%。此外，第二季民間投資成長3.0%，其中非住宅投資成長 8.4%，顯示 AI 資料中心、軟體與
研發支出仍持續擴張。另一方面，第二季進口大幅成長11.5%，增幅主要集中於電信設備、半導體及
工業設備等資本財，反映企業資本支出需求仍相當強勁。
能源價格回落有助通膨降溫，但整體物價壓力仍不容忽視。6 月 PCE 與核心 PCE 月增率同步放
緩 ， 其中整體PCE 轉為月減 0.1%， 反映能源價格明顯回落 ， 足以抵銷其他商品與服務價格上漲壓力，
年增率為 3.7%，仍高於 Fed 目標。核心 PCE 月增率亦降至 0.1%，創近數月低點；其中，聯準會高
度關注的超級核心服務通膨出現降溫 ， 主要受投資組合管理費漲幅由4.5%大幅降至 0.6%， 以及旅館
住宿與機票價格回落帶動。不過，醫療保健價格仍維持穩健增長，且部分商品價格持續受到關稅影響
而上揚，加上美伊戰事仍未落幕，通膨風險依舊存在。

本輪由 AI 投資帶動的「AI 通膨」 ，對整體CPI 的直接推升有限，卻有助於川普推動「美國製造」
政策目標。2024 年以來 ， 全球雲端服務業者大幅擴張AI 資本支出 ， 帶動高階晶片 、記憶體需求急升，
不過，電視、電腦周邊及手機合計占美國CPI 權重偏低，即使相關產品價格上漲，對整體通膨的直接
影響仍相對有限。相較之下，AI 通膨對產業結構與供應鏈在地化的影響更值得關注。台積電毛利率
上升同時也擴大在美國資本支出 、美光也在記憶體價格回升後重新啟動紐約廠建設、輝達則進一步投
資光通訊與先進封裝業者 ， 協助相關產能在美國落地 。 唯有當價格與需求長期維持在足以支撐獲利的
水準，企業才有能力承擔高額資本支出，並推動製造業供應鏈回美擴張。
目前標普 500 指數續創新高，本益比已升至 25.8 倍、股價淨值比達 5.8 倍，短期評價偏高。雖
然美國科技業者目前仍居 AI 產業領先地位，但AI 技術與競爭格局變化快速，領先廠商尚未與後續追
趕者明顯拉開差距。短期仍需留意市場過熱與估值偏高風險，建議以長線角度分批布局。

歐洲股市：經濟溫和復甦，評價仍具吸引力
七月歐股受伊朗戰爭不確定性干擾而震盪。泛歐 600 指數中，能源與金融股表現相對強勢，包括
殼牌、匯豐、道達爾能源、BP、畢爾巴鄂比斯開銀行、聯合聖保羅銀行及法國巴黎銀行等；相較之
下 ， 半導體 、 科技硬體與部分防禦型消費股表現偏弱 ， 包括艾司摩爾 、 英飛凌 、 意法半導體、諾基亞、
雀巢等。整體市場焦點由科技股回歸能源與金融族群。
歐元區第 2 季 GDP 初值季增率回升至 0.4%，顯示經濟動能在第 1 季停滯後逐步修復。不過，成
員國表現仍明顯分化：西班牙季增 0.7%，持續領先主要經濟體，主要受惠於服務業復甦與觀光需求

回升；德國 GDP 季增率則放緩至 0.2%，反映製造業疲弱與出口需求不足仍限制復甦力道；法國則
由前季萎縮轉為成長 0.2%，顯示內需逐步改善。整體而言，第 2 季 GDP 回升主要受消費改善、投
資回穩及政府支出支撐，但復甦力道仍屬溫和。
7 月歐元區通膨率受能源價格反彈影響 ， 小幅回升至2.9%， 已連續5 個月高於 ECB 的 2.0%目標 ，
但仍低於 5 月 3.2%的高點。核心通膨則升至 2.5%，主要受服務業與非能源工業品價格同步走揚帶
動，其中服務業通膨由3.2%升至 3.3%，非能源工業品由 0.7%升至 0.9%；食品與菸酒年增率則續降
至 1.2%，反映食品價格降溫趨勢延續。整體而言，物價壓力僅溫和回升，尚未出現全面加速跡象。
ECB 在七月利率決策會議維持政策利率 2.25%不變，重申將採取逐次會議、數據依賴的決策模
式，不預先承諾未來利率路徑，官方仍維持經濟成長風險偏下行的評估。雖然歐元區整體通膨較前期
高點放緩 ， 但中東衝突再度升溫 ， 推升原油與天然氣價格 ， 加上目前全球能源與天然氣庫存水位偏低 ，

未來能源價格變化及其對通膨的傳導效果，仍將是市場關注重點。ECB 刻意未恢復前瞻指引，並持續
強調後續數據的重要性 ， 反映其希望在高度不確定環境下保留政策彈性 ， 因此不排除9 月升息的可能 。
7 月歐元區綜合 PMI 回升至 51.9，重返榮枯線之上並創 5 個月新高，整體景氣動能有所改善。
其中，服務業 PMI 升至 51.6，受惠於需求回升而重返擴張區間；製造業 PMI 亦由 51.4 升至 51.9，
加上新訂單 5 個月來首度恢復成長，進一步支撐景氣修復。
歐洲經濟整體呈現溫和復甦 ， 泛歐600 指數成分股多為具備全球布局優勢的產業龍頭 。 目前指數
本益比約 17.5 倍、股價淨值比約 2.5 倍，評價仍屬合理。雖然能源價格高檔與通膨再升溫風險仍需
留意，但在評價合理、伊朗戰爭有望於第三季落幕的背景下，歐股仍適合作為長線分批布局標的。

日本股市：日圓匯率短線急升，股市評價合理
七月日股受地緣政治風險與全球科技股回檔影響，日經 225 指數劇烈震盪。盤面上，金融、外銷
與大型權值股表現相對強勢，包括三菱 UFJ 金融集團、日立製作所、索尼、豐田、三井住友金融、
東京海上控股、瑞穗金融、三菱商事等；半導體與電子零組件族群則相對承壓，包括東京威力科創、
村田製作所、鎧俠、瑞薩、Disco、信越化學等。整體市場焦點由科技股轉向外銷股與升息預期受惠
的金融股。
7 月東京 CPI 年增率自前月的 1.7%攀升至 2.0%、東京核心 CPI（扣除生鮮食品） 年增率為1.9%、

扣除食品與能源後的 CPI 年增率則為 1.3%。商品價格年增率由前月的 2.3%升至 2.9%，是通膨回升
的主要原因；服務價格年增率則小幅升至 1.2%，顯示薪資成長仍推升服務價格。分項來看，本月通
膨上行主要來自能源與公用事業費用，反映電費與瓦斯費價格上漲；同時，食品價格年增率也由前月
的 3.8%回升至 4.0%。展望後市，日本政府 7~9 月重啟電費補貼，將抑制能源價格。不過，近期國
際油價再度攀高，可能抵消部分政策效果。
日本央行七月利率決策會議維持政策利率不變，委員以 8 比 1 同意將政策利率維持於 1.0%。僅
高田創委員主張將政策利率調高至 1.25%。就景氣現況來看，日本經濟仍呈現溫和復甦態勢，儘管部
分經濟活動受到中東局勢影響而略顯疲弱，但出口與工業生產大致保持平穩。受惠於全球 AI 相關需
求強勁成長，企業固定投資仍溫和增加；同時，私人消費在就業與所得環境改善支撐下仍具韌性。日
銀也將 2026 財年 GDP 年增率預估由前次的 0.5%小幅上調至 0.6%，2027 財年由 0.7%上調至 0.8%。

近期美日聯手干預日圓匯率，日本財務大臣片山皋月表示，已協同美國財政部實施買進日圓的
干預措施，以因應近來日圓匯率過度波動及失序走勢，且不排除進一步採取協同干預。美國財長貝森
特也表示，將毫不猶豫參與進一步的聯合日圓干預行動，並強烈支持日本採取果斷措施，修正日圓嚴
重低估的問題。美國總統川普則稱，日本需要一些幫助。此次為日本與美國 15 年來首次就日圓採取
聯合行動，兩國共同干預匯市確實對日圓形成支撐，突襲式干預也有助引發投資人空單回補，暫時延
續日圓升值趨勢。不過，目前日美政策利率差距仍高達 2.5%，若日本央行無法加快升息步伐，中長
期匯價重新回歸利差基本面，日圓仍可能重拾偏弱格局。
2026 年來日股 TOPIX 指數受惠於 AI 題材 、 高市擴大財政刺激與強化軍事安全等政策利多支撐，
儘管指數已明顯上漲，目前本益比約 19.5 倍、股價淨值比約 1.8 倍，評價仍屬合理，可利用市場震
盪機會逢低分批布局。

台灣股市：AI 出口與接單動能續強，8 月聚焦財報兌現與供應鏈擴散
7 月台股因前期漲幅較大、科技股評價偏高，加上市場重新檢視AI 投資回收期、企業獲利能否符
合高預期，外資調節、融資去化與槓桿資金停損進一步放大波動，資金也由高評價電子股轉向較具防
禦性的族群。不過，這波修正有助消化過高評價與浮動籌碼，降低市場過熱風險；後續隨雲端業者財
報持續驗證 AI 資本支出，半導體、伺服器及先進封裝需求延續，市場焦點可望逐步回歸企業獲利與
產業基本面 。6 月景氣對策信號升至 41 分 、 連續第7 個月亮紅燈 ， 製造業銷售與加班工時同步轉強，

顯示 AI 動能已由出口延伸至生產、設備投資與勞動投入。雖然產業間景氣感受仍有落差，但下半年
電子產品備貨需求、半導體資本支出及 AI 基礎建設持續推進，仍可望支撐出口與企業獲利，為後續
盤勢提供正向動能。
當前臺灣經濟正處於生產擴張的黃金期。最新公布的總體經濟數據與半導體龍頭營收呈現全面共
振，印證全球 AI 資本支出（CapEx）並非流於故事題材，而是實質轉化為台廠的訂單與獲利。儘管
全球金融市場短期受到地緣政治干擾與美債殖利率波動影響 ， 引發台股在高檔震盪 ， 但主計總處大幅
上修 2026 年 GDP 預測至 9.64%。國發會公布 6 月景氣對策信號綜合判斷分數為 41 分，較 5 月
增加 2 分，連續第 7 個月呈現代表景氣熱絡的紅燈，為史上第二長的連續紅燈紀錄，顯示台灣景氣
並非單月跳升，而是已形成一段相對持續的高檔擴張週期。
展望 8 月，台灣景氣仍有望維持高檔，但景氣分數的邊際變化將比燈號本身更值得關注。全球
AI 基礎建設持續擴張，加上下半年進入消費性電子備貨旺季，預期有利半導體、AI 伺服器、先進封

裝、散熱、電源及高速傳輸供應鏈；國內主要半導體業者提高資本支出，也將持續帶動設備、材料及
廠務工程需求。另一方面，景氣分數已接近紅燈區間上緣 45 分，未來即使出口與生產維持強勁，受
到比較基期墊高影響，分數未必持續上升，因此不宜將分數持平或小幅回落直接解讀為景氣反轉。
投資策略上可維持對 AI 供應鏈的正向看法，但選股宜從題材轉向獲利品質。除先進製程外，8
月可提高對先進封裝、測試介面、AI 伺服器組裝、散熱、及記憶體的關注。IDC 預估 2026 年全球半
導體市場規模將達 1.29 兆美元、年增52.8%，資料中心半導體營收達 4,771 億美元，意味著AI 投資
正由 GPU 延伸至記憶體、網路與系統級零組件。不過，若股價已提前反映高成長，8 月仍應留意財
測不及預期、匯率、成本上升及資金集中度過高所引發的波動。

中國股市：復甦力道偏緩，科技自主為結構性題材但仍待驗證
7 月中國經濟延續 「總量放緩 、 科技製造相對抗跌」 的分化格局 。2026 年上半年 GDP 年增 4.7%，
第二季成長率由第一季的 5.0%放緩至 4.3%；6 月規模以上工業增加值年增 5.3%，其中製造業成長
6.0%，電腦、通信及其他電子設備製造業成長 15.7%。數據顯示 AI 與電子設備提供局部支撐，但內
需疲弱與房地產調整仍未見明顯改善，整體復甦動能偏緩，成長結構對少數產業的依賴度偏高。
科技產業方面，7 月市場焦點轉向半導體設備、AI 模型與記憶體的自主化，惟題材面成分仍高於

基本面。據媒體報導，中國已開始製造國產浸沒式 DUV 微影設備，首批系統預計供應中芯國際、華
虹半導體及長鑫科技 ， 但2026 年產量可能僅約 5 台 ， 且效能 、 可靠度與量產驗證仍明顯落後ASML，
短期象徵意義大於實際產能貢獻 。 後續能否降低成熟製程與部分多重曝光製程對進口設備的依賴 ， 仍
取決於良率與客戶端驗證結果，對國產蝕刻、薄膜沉積、檢測及零組件的實質拉動亦需時間觀察。
AI 軟體端，月之暗面於 7 月推出 Kimi K3，模型具 2.8 兆參數、100 萬 Token 上下文及原生視覺
理解能力，並採開放權重策略，反映中國大模型競爭已由低價推論延伸至模型能力與開發者生態。惟
開放權重的商業模式尚未證明可持續獲利，應用落地速度亦存在不確定性。若相關應用推展順利，或
有利國產雲端服務 、 資料中心 、 伺服器與推論晶片需求 ； 但模型效率提升同時可能壓抑算力採購規模 ，
市場對 AI 投資的檢視標準將轉向使用效率與變現能力。
記憶體方面 ， 長鑫科技正由一般DRAM 逐步向 DDR5、LPDDR5X 及 HBM3 延伸 ， 市場研究 「估

計」 其2026 年底 DRAM 月產能可能接近 35 萬片晶圓，惟該預估未經證實，實際良率與產品組合仍
待確認 。 國產DUV 若逐步導入其產線 ， 或可提高設備取得的自主性 ， 但導入時程與穩定度均具變數。
整體而言，8 月中國市場可留意半導體設備、國產記憶體、AI 模型、資料中心與工業自動化，惟評價
已部分反映預期，投資判斷宜以技術驗證、訂單落地與實際營收貢獻為依據，避免過度追逐自主替代
題材。
展望 8 月，政策環境預計仍以穩增長及培育新動能為主，但政策效果傳導有限，市場表現料將持
續分化。中國上半年新動能，包括高端製造、數位經濟與現代服務業，對經濟成長的貢獻超過四成；
第三季製造業雖有 AI 相關產品、綠色科技出口及政策支持，惟房地產、基礎建設投資及中小企業需
求疲弱仍是主要壓抑因素。

拉美市場：巴西通膨改善支撐市場表現，惟總統大選不確定性仍存
7 月全球股市普遍面臨回檔，然而拉美股市表現卻相對抗跌，主因巴西市場先前已因降息預期收
斂而出現修正，而近期巴西通膨數據及通膨預期出現改善，使市場對於巴西通膨問題的擔憂有所緩
解，也帶動巴西指數表現相對硬挺，並為整體拉美市場指數提供支撐。
7 月巴西通膨數據出現改善，主要來自食品與燃料價格回落。7 月巴西 IPCA-15 消費者物價指
數月增 0.06%，明顯低於市場預期的 0.22%，年增率亦由 6 月的 4.80% 降至 4.52%。其中，家庭
食品價格由 6 月月增 0.87% 轉為月減 1.14%；燃料價格整體月減 0.90%， 汽油價格月減 0.68%，

帶動整體通膨放緩，並降低市場對近期巴西物價再度急升的疑慮。
此外，本次巴西通膨數據改善亦反映在核心通膨與服務通膨上，使優於預期的數據更接近結構性
通膨改善。五種核心通膨指標平均月增 0.21%，年增率由 4.45% 降至 4.35%。同時，服務通膨月
增 0.41%，低於市場預期的 0.59%，年增率也由 6.25% 降至 5.95%；然而，勞力密集型服務年增
率仍由 7.06% 升至 7.30%，顯示通膨壓力尚未全面消退，後續仍需持續觀察。
另一方面，儘管巴西通膨數據改善 ， 仍未逆轉市場對巴西央行降息幅度受限的預期。截至 7 月 27
日的巴西央行每週市場調查顯示，2026 年市場通膨預期較前期下降 3 個基點至 5.12%，但 2027
年仍上升 2 個基點至 4.22%，2028 年亦連續第二週上升 2 個基點至 3.80%，2029 年則維持
3.50%；四個年度皆持續高於巴西央行 3.0%的通膨目標 。此外 ，市場對 2026、2027、2028 及 2029
年底巴西基準利率的預期，亦分別維持在 14.00%、12.00%、10.50%及 10.00%，顯示市場對通膨

的疑慮仍限制巴西利率正常化的速度與幅度。
除了通膨之外，巴西總統大選是影響拉美市場的另一項關鍵因素。巴西民調機構 Nexus 於 7 月
全國民調顯示，在總統大選第一輪中，魯拉以 42% 領先波索納洛的 33%，與 7 月中旬前次調查
的 42% 對 34% 大致相同；第二輪則為 47% 對 43%，魯拉仍領先 4 個百分點。兩輪調查均由
魯拉領先，顯示其整體勝率仍較高。此外，包含 Datafolha、Genial／Quaest 等民調皆顯示目前魯
拉贏面較高。由於魯拉並非市場偏好的候選人，其較高的連任機率可能壓抑巴西股市表現。因此，儘
管近期巴西通膨降溫有助支撐整體拉美市場行情，巴西選舉風險仍可能抵銷部分利多；同時，在美伊
衝突仍具不確定性的情況下，巴西降息行情亦料將受到壓抑，後市表現預期將落後於全球市場。

科技類股：CSP 廠商資本支出持續加碼，市場更加關注獲利轉化能力
全球科技產業的主要成長動能仍集中於 AI 基礎建設，四大雲端服務供應商資本支出均維持高檔
或進一步上修，顯示資料中心、AI 伺服器、網路設備及雲端容量的擴建週期尚未降溫。Microsoft、
Amazon 與 Google 的雲端業務均維持強勁成長，且訂單、產能需求與客戶使用量持續增加，反映企
業導入生成式 AI、主權 AI 及 AI 工廠的趨勢仍在深化。市場焦點也逐漸由單一 AI 晶片出貨，延伸至
高速網路、記憶體、CPU、電力與散熱等整體資料中心架構。只要 CSP 廠商未明顯下修資本支出，

AI 硬體供應鏈的訂單能見度便可望維持，NVIDIA、AMD 及相關半導體與設備廠商仍是本輪科技投
資循環的主要受惠者。
不過，市場對科技公司的評價標準正由投入多少資本支出轉向能否將投資轉化為營收、獲利與現
金流。Microsoft 在大幅增加資本支出的同時，營運現金流與自由現金流仍維持成長，Google 雲端
業務的獲利也明顯提升 ， 說明部分AI 投資已逐步進入商業化階段 ；Amazon 則受惠 AWS 成長加速，
雲端業務持續扮演主要獲利來源。相較之下，Meta 與特斯拉因 AI 研發、基礎建設及新事業投入快
速增加，面臨費用上升、利潤率下降與自由現金流承壓的問題。未來科技股表現可能進一步分化，市
場將更重視 AI 收入成長 、 產能利用率 、 投資回收期及折舊費用 ， 而非單純將高額資本支出視為利多。
從供應鏈角度來看，AI 需求正由 GPU 逐步擴散至 CPU、記憶體、晶圓代工、類比晶片與工業半
導體。Intel 資料中心與 AI 業務明顯加速，顯示 CPU 在 AI 伺服器的資料處理、系統控制與通用運算

架構中仍具重要地位。記憶體價格上升雖有利於相關製造商，但也推高 CSP 建置成本，促使雲端業
者重新檢視採購效率與系統配置。整體而言，科技產業有機會由少數 AI 核心企業領漲，逐步轉為伺
服器、網通、儲存、電源、散熱及工業半導體共同受惠的擴散型成長。
展望後續，AI 仍是科技產業最具確定性的投資主軸，但高成長也伴隨高估值與高資本密集度風
險。NVIDIA 的 Blackwell 出貨、Rubin 平台時程、高階記憶體供應與中國市場需求，將是觀察 AI
硬體循環的重要指標；Apple、Meta 與特斯拉則需證明 AI 功能、新裝置及自動化應用能夠轉化為實
際收入。目前 CSP 訂單與雲端需求仍未出現明顯降溫，科技產業基本面維持正向，但隨著折舊、能
源、記憶體及研發成本陸續上升，企業獲利差異可能擴大。未來較具優勢的企業，將不只是擁有先進
技術或積極投入 AI， 而是能建立穩定商業模式 ， 並將AI 投資有效轉化為營收 、 利潤與現金流的公司。

能源類股：美伊衝突帶動油價波動，實體供應仍未見改善
7 月油價波動劇烈，主因美伊衝突與停火談判反覆。美伊在 7 月中旬恢復互相攻擊、美國重啟
對伊朗港口的海上封鎖，且荷莫茲海峽再度發生商船遇襲事件，帶動布蘭特原油一度重返每桶 100
美元以上；然而其後美方暫停攻擊並重啟外交談判後，油價又回落至約 80 美元，這顯示當前油價
變動主要反映市場對於衝突升降溫的預期，而非實際供應已恢復正常。
彭博分析指出，目前石油實物市場尚未出現明顯改善，荷莫茲海峽可觀察到的油輪數量仍遠低於
戰前，許多船隻通行時仍持續關閉船舶自動識別系統，且保險公司、租船人及船東也維持審慎態度，

顯示商業航運、海運保險、運費及出口流量均未恢復正常。航運資料公司 Windward 7 月 30 日的
船舶追蹤也顯示，荷莫茲海峽當日僅有 7 艘船舶進入波斯灣、4 艘離開，整體運量相較於戰前仍有
極大的落差，故一旦美伊談判受阻，地緣政治風險溢價仍有迅速回升的可能性。
另一方面，胡塞武裝宣布將封鎖沙烏地阿拉伯有關的船舶運輸，並對紅海曼德海峽通行發出威
脅，也加劇油價上行風險。彭博指出，沙烏地阿拉伯雖可利用東西輸油管，將原先在荷莫茲海峽受阻
的每日約 500 萬桶原油運往紅海沿岸的延布港，但經由紅海運往亞洲的船舶仍須通過曼德海峽，因
此原油出口風險只是由波斯灣轉移至紅海。船舶追蹤公司 Windward 截至 8 月 3 日的統計顯示，
7 月 20 日後曼德海峽船隻流量下降 22%， 油輪更減少 40%，顯示經紅海的替代路線仍面臨威脅。
然而 ， 高油價帶來的需求破壞也會限制油價上漲空間 。OPEC 7 月月報將 2026 年全球石油需求
量下修至每日 1.0594 億桶，需求年增量由每日 97 萬桶降至 78 萬桶；2027 年需求年增量雖上

修至每日 194 萬桶，但上修主因 2026 年基期較低的緣故，並非終端需求出現改善。
在此背景下，彭博研究將油價分為三種情境：若外交協議落地、荷莫茲航運與保險恢復正常，布
蘭特原油可能回到每桶 65 至 80 美元；若戰事僅暫停、實物供應持續受阻，則較符合每桶 90 至
110 美元的衝突僵局情境；若荷莫茲與曼德海峽同時受阻，油價可能升至每桶 140 美元以上。
展望 8 月，川普宣布將和伊朗展開新一輪談判，儘管隨即遭到伊朗否認，且荷莫茲海峽仍處於受阻
狀態 ， 然而我們認為隨著期中選舉時程逼近 ， 川普將可能暫緩對伊朗的行動 ， 藉此壓低油價挽救選情 ，
在此情境下能源類股將會轉趨區間震盪整理，目前中性看待後市表現。

黃金類股：央行配置需求仍強，惟 ETF 資金流出壓抑短線表現
7 月金價保持在區間內震盪整理，儘管美元走強及美債殖利率攀升持續為金價帶來壓力，然而來
自全球央行的買盤也如我們先前所料的為金價帶來了有力的支撐。
世界黃金協會報告指出，2026 年第二季，全球黃金總需求為 1,269 噸，與 2025 年同期持平；
累計 2026 年上半年，全球黃金總需求年增 2%至 2,522 噸，需求金額創下 3,800 億美元新高。與此
同時 ，2026 年第二季倫敦金銀市場協會黃金午後定盤價平均為每盎司 4,506.29 美元 ， 雖較第一季下

跌 8%，但仍較 2025 年同期上漲 37%。這顯示第二季金價雖自高檔回落，但全球黃金需求並未同步
轉弱，尤其央行買盤回升更成為支撐市場的主要力量。
第二季全球央行合計購買 289 噸黃金，歷經第一季的放緩後，購金規模再次重新回到過去四年
的高檔水準。高盛分析則預估，全球央行 5 月購金量達到 81 噸，其中中國購買 48 噸，是當月最大
的可識別買家；經季節調整後，全球央行近三個月月均購金量達到 67 噸，約為 2022 年前每月 17
噸平均值的四倍 。 上述數據顯示 ， 即使在金價走勢低迷的狀況下，全球央行對於黃金的承接力道仍強 。
進一步觀察央行持續購金的原因，官方貨幣與金融機構論壇（OMFIF）2026 年針對 74 家央行
及 16 家公共退休與主權基金進行的調查顯示，儲備多元化仍是央行持有黃金最主要的原因。此外，
有 51%的受訪者將防範地緣政治風險列為考量，比例較 2024 年增加 11 個百分點；同時，79%的受
訪央行認為全球貨幣體系正朝向多極化發展 。調查結果顯示， 各國央行正逐步降低對單一儲備貨幣的

依賴，並增持黃金以因應制裁、資產凍結及貨幣體系分裂風險，由此也可見，本波全球央行買盤更接
近長期儲備調整，而非短線對於金價的追逐。
然而對於利率較為敏感的私人投資資金 ， 降低黃金配置趨勢仍持續為金價帶來明顯壓力。 世界黃
金協會數據顯示，2026 年第二季全球黃金 ETF 淨流出 45 噸，6 月單月再流出 89 億美元；其中，北
美黃金 ETF 上半年累計流出 77 億美元。造成北美資金流出的主因，在於聯準會態度偏向鷹派，加上
美伊衝突推升通膨疑慮，帶動實質利率與美元走高，進而提高持有黃金的機會成本。
展望後市，儘管短線上金價表現持續承壓，然而中長線看，當前市場已定價到明年底前 Fed 將
升息 2 碼的鷹派走勢，若後續美伊衝突有所緩和，使市場升息預期降溫，黃金 ETF 資金賣壓有望出
現改善， 屆時搭配央行長期買盤 ，黃金仍有望重啟新一波漲勢，故我們對黃金類股後市維持中性看法 。

固定收益：Fed 鷹派暫停推升長率，投資仍以 3~10 年期美債/投級債為主
FOMC 7/29 以 9 比 3 票將聯邦基金利率目標區間維持於 3.50%～3.75%。Hammack、Kashkari
及 Logan 三名委員反對維持利率不變，主張本次即升息 1 碼。會後聲明指出美國經濟活動仍以穩健
速度擴張，資本投資及生產力成長強勁，但通膨仍高於 2%目標，部分反映能源等供給衝擊。
Fed 主席 Warsh 強調，本次按兵不動並非聯準會行動遲緩或政策慣性，並重申不存在高於 2%
的 「軟性通膨目標」 。超過五年的高通膨不可能因單月物價小幅下降便獲得解決，聯準會將持續履行

物價穩定責任，必要時不會猶豫採取行動。華許同時指出，距離上次會議的 42 天內，美債殖利率曲
線各期限的名目與實質殖利率均明顯上升，部分升幅已位居近 20 年的前十分位數。他認為，聯準會
減少前瞻指引後，市場開始依據經濟數據自行調整價格。此番談話並非表示聯準會受制於市場，而是
暗示金融市場利率上升已產生部分緊縮效果，使本次不必立即升息。華許指出，AI 相關高科技設備
及軟體投資近四季增長率接近 20%，有助於製造業產出及未來生產力，但記憶體、邏輯晶片及相關
AI 基礎建設價格亦同步上升。FOMC 已開始討論，相關漲價究竟只是特定產業的供給衝擊，或可能
演變為更廣泛的通膨壓力。
債市對此次 FOMC 決議的反應呈現長端領跌、殖利率曲線明顯陡峭化。此一走勢顯示，市場雖
因 Fed 未立即升息而降低短端利率壓力，卻同步提高對能源通膨、財政供給、期限溢酬及政策可信
度的風險定價。利率期貨對 9 月升息的定價在決議後一度升至約 75%，稍後回落至約 6 成，代表市
場並未排除升息，而是等待後續通膨與就業數據確認。8 月沒有例行 FOMC 利率會議，債市定價將

更加依賴經濟數據。由於 Warsh 減少前瞻指引，單一數據對升息機率及短端殖利率的影響可能較過
去放大，預期 8 月殖利率仍將維持高檔震盪。
8 月債市的核心矛盾在於「殖利率已具吸引力，但長端風險尚未完全穩定」。我們認為偏高的殖
利率可提供較佳息收及價格緩衝；若油價維持高檔、核心通膨降溫停滯或美國公債供給增加， 20～
30 年期債券仍可能承受期限溢酬上升壓力。貝萊德亦指出，目前美債實質殖利率已具吸引力，但收
益機會仍應透過存續期間控制與券種選擇取得，而非全面延長年期。
在未來聯準會減少前瞻指引 、 利率路徑更依賴數據環境下 ， 市場對聯準會政策預期的調整頻率將
提高，預期債券市場波動性亦將隨之升溫。投資人對 Fed 升息疑慮或導致短天期債券殖利率出現較
大波動，且存續期間過短亦面臨再投資風險；長天期債券又有期限溢酬偏高、政府財政惡化及公債供
給增加等風險。因此，建議債券布局可聚焦 3 至 10 年期美國公債及投等債，不僅能提供相對具吸
引力的息收，亦能降低短端政策波動及長端期限溢酬風險。

個股報告

毅嘉(2402) 
光通產品高速增長，海外產能持續擴張 
投資評等 目標價 
逢低買進 $71 
前次投資建議：無

報告日期：2026.10.27 研 究 員：邱岳霖

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 26Q4 光通訊量產，惟缺料與淡季影響營運動能，EPS 0.41元。 
➢ AI 光通訊放量帶動營收倍增，mSAP量產為毛利率改善關鍵，2027 年 EPS 3.1 元，考量公
司切入高速成長之光通賽道，未來幾年營運將高速增長，兆豐投顧給予目標價 71 元(23X 
27(F)PER)，潛在上漲空間 23.9%，投資評等為「逢低買進」 。

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/10/22 收盤價： 57.30 ➢ 光通訊散熱型金屬外殼已於26Q3量產 ，FlipChip打件與
液冷測漏板於26Q4導入量產，預期光通訊營收佔比由
1%升至6%，然MLCC、DSP、PCB三項缺料影響，同
時進入消費性產品淡季，預估毅嘉 26Q4營收32.7億
元，QoQ -4.3%，毛利率14.5%，季增0.2ppt，稅後淨
利1.3億元，QoQ +23.9%，EPS 0.41元。 
➢ 展望後市，AI光通訊將成為毅嘉主要成長動能，預期AI
相關營收佔比將由26Q4之6%躍升至2027年40%， 係因
800G光收發模組需求持續強勁，目前主要客戶明年總
需求為27-30KK，此外1.6T/3.2T世代將於2028至2029
年接續拉升，帶動Flip Chip打件需求大幅擴大，而毅嘉
持續在馬來西亞擴產，雖帶動營收顯著提升，不過因採
turnkey出貨，料件佔BOM cost約80%至90%，故毛利
率僅約5%至8%，將稀釋整體毛利率。後續改善關鍵在
於mSAP高密度電路板，目前已啟動客戶樣品打樣，預
期27Q3量產，毛利率可達40%以上；此外散熱事業營
收倍增並轉虧為盈，攜手杰華精密切入光模組散熱外
殼，加上IMS延伸至儲能與機器人應用，將使產品組合
逐步轉佳。綜上所述，兆豐投顧預估毅嘉 2027年營收
248.9億元，YoY +94.9%，毛利率11.5%，年減2.5ppt，
稅後淨利9.5億元，YoY +159%，EPS 3.1元。考量公
司切入高速成長之光通賽道 ，未來幾年營運將高速增
長，兆豐投顧給予目標價71元(23X 27(F)PER) ，潛在
上漲空間23.9%，投資評等為「逢低買進」 。

資料來源：CMoney；兆豐投顧預估

營 運 分 析

毅嘉科技為軟板及機構整合廠  
毅嘉科技成立於 1983 年，初期以生產手機按鍵為主，於 2000 年掛牌上市
並跨足軟板製造領域，2020 年延伸至硬板打件並在跨入車用模組，2024 年
將戰略重心擴張至光通訊領域，從早期供應光收發模組用軟硬結合板，2026
年切入光收發模組 Flip Chip SMT，於 26Q3 完成認證並於 26Q4 放量生產，
預計 2027 年再導入 mSAP 高密度電路板，預期 27Q3 開始量產，此外毅嘉
於 26Q2 投資光通訊零組件廠商杰華精密(持股 46.25%)跨入光模組金屬外
殼。

毅嘉現主營軟板及機構整合業務：26Q3 應用別營收組成為車用 62%(智能座
艙 40%，連接板 19%，內飾件 3%)，其他消費 10%，遊戲 9%，穿戴設備
8%，其他產品 7%，手機 3%，光通訊 1%(26Q4 預估升至 6%)。生產基地
位於台灣林口廠(研發中心、散熱模組、IMS 研發中心)、大陸蘇州廠(車用軟
板、空板、 SMT、Mini LED)、大陸中山廠(穿戴裝置)、馬來西亞檳城及吉
打廠(25Q3 完工，產品為機構件、光通訊、車用、儲能 )，越南及匈牙利設
有銷售據點，北美據點評估中(德州)。

- 3 - 
個股報告 
26Q3 營收創今年新高，然新廠成本與匯損壓抑獲利，EPS 0.33元 
毅嘉(2402)公告 26Q3財報：營收 34.2 億元，QoQ +4.4%/YoY +18.4%，再
創近年新高，主要受惠車用及消費性電子產品需求成長帶動，毛利率 14.3%，
季增 0.1ppt，然因馬來西亞新廠初期營運成本與費用增加，營業利益較上季
衰退 10.5%，同時受人民幣升值產生匯兌損失，故稅後淨利 1 億元，QoQ 
-16.0%/YoY -45.1%，EPS 0.33元。

26Q4 光通訊量產，惟缺料與淡季影響營運動能，EPS 0.41元 
光通訊散熱型金屬外殼已於 26Q3 量產，FlipChip打件與液冷測漏板於 26Q4
導入量產，預期光通訊營收佔比由 1%升至 6%，然 MLCC、DSP、PCB 三
項缺料影響，同時進入消費性產品淡季，兆豐投顧預估毅嘉 26Q4 營收 32.7
億元，QoQ -4.3%/YoY +20.0%，毛利率 14.5%，季增 0.2ppt，年減 5.3ppt，
稅後淨利 1.3 億元，QoQ +23.9%/YoY -32.9%，EPS 0.41元。

AI 光通訊放量帶動營收倍增，mSAP量產為毛利率改善關鍵，2027年 EPS 
3.1元 
展望後市，AI 光通訊將成為毅嘉主要成長動能，預期 AI 相關營收佔比將由
26Q4之 6%躍升至 2027 年 40%，係因 800G 光收發模組需求持續強勁，目
前主要客戶明年總需求為 27-30KK，此外 1.6T/3.2T 世代將於 2028 至 2029
年接續拉升，帶動 Flip Chip 打件需求大幅擴大，而毅嘉持續在馬來西亞擴
產，Flip Chip 產線將由今年 3條提升至 2027 年 8-10條，2028 年進一步擴
充至 24條，雖帶動營收顯著提升，不過因採 turnkey 出貨，料件佔 BOM cost
約 80%至 90%，故毛利率僅約 5%至 8%，將稀釋整體毛利率。後續改善關
鍵在於 mSAP高密度電路板，目前已啟動客戶樣品打樣，預期 27Q3量產，
毛利率可達 40%以上；此外散熱事業營收倍增並轉虧為盈，攜手杰華精密切
入光模組散熱外殼，加上IMS 延伸至儲能與機器人應用，將使產品組合逐步
轉佳。綜上所述，兆豐投顧預估毅嘉2027 年營收 248.9 億元，YoY +94.9%，
毛利率 11.5%，年減2.5ppt，稅後淨利9.5 億元，YoY +159%，EPS 3.1 元。

Document 134: 261029_daiwa_Delta

Source PDF: all report pdf/261029_daiwa_Delta.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We provide our latest views on Delta after its 3Q26 results 
call on 29 October.

What’s the impact: Business outlook. During the results call, Delta 
remained positive on AI monetisation after its analysis of Anthropic’s S-1 
filing. Management flagged shortages of components, power and skilled 
electricians and slower-than-expected data centre buildout to be potential 
hiccups in the process; however, it still sees strong underlying demand. As 
such, we still expect sequential revenue growth in both 4Q26E and 1Q27E 
as component shortages had deferred 3Q26 demand. On rising material 
costs, Delta noted that BoM costs have increased by 2% as of 3Q26 (vs. 
early 2026), with the company gradually passing these costs through to 
end customers in 4Q26 and 1Q27. We believe the main gross margin 
driver will likely still be the absence of inventory write-offs and a higher 
power mix in 4Q26E. We forecast a gross margin recovery to 36% and a 
record operating margin of 18.5% in 4Q26. As for long-term growth, we 
maintain our investment theses that DC-DC module and HVDC adoption 
will become key growth drivers for Delta in 2027 and 2028, respectively.

3Q26 results missed on gross margin. Delta's 3Q26 earnings were 
TWD23.5bn (EPS: TWD9.04), missing our estimate of TWD27.3bn and 
Bloomberg consensus estimate of TWD29.6bn by 14% and 21%, 
respectively. We attribute the weakness mainly to lower-than-expected 
gross margin, driven by: 1) higher-than-expected inventory write-offs amid 
product shipment hiccups; 2) a one-off provision for customer claims, with 
the related loss likely to ultimately be borne by the supplier; and 3) rising 
material costs. Based on our calculation, Delta recognised c.TWD4bn of 
inventory provision loss in 3Q26, which reduced gross margin by 2pp. 
Excluding inventory provision loss, gross margin was c.35.8%, close to our 
previous estimate of 36.3%. 3Q26 revenue reached TWD197.1bn (up 8% 
QoQ and 31% YoY), which was also negatively affected by product 
shipment hiccups (vs. our previous estimate of 10% QoQ growth).

What we recommend: We trim our 2026-28E EPS by 2-6% to reflect lower 
revenue assumptions due to the impact of component shortages. We 
reaffirm our Buy (1) call but trim our 12M TP to TWD3,000 (from 
TWD3,088) based on an unchanged target PER of 49x (high-end of its 3-
year range of 11-50x) applied to our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Key 
downside risk: lower-than-expected demand.

How we differ: Our 2028E revenue is 12% above the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely on our more bullish assumption regarding HVDC 
adoption.

29 October 2026

3Q26 review: margin likely bottomed-out

➢ Gross margin to recover on lower inventory provisions in 4Q26 
➢ Addressable market remains intact despite some near-term hiccups   
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating; trimming 12M TP to TWD3,000

Company profile 
Founded in 1971, Delta Electronics has been the global leader in switching power supply solutions since 
2002 and DC brushless fans since 2006. Its business covers 3 key areas: power electronics (eg, power 
supplies, thermal management products and passive components), automation (eg, controllers, robot 
arms and building management systems), and infrastructure (eg, datacentre infrastructure, networking 
and EV charging).

### Page 5

5

Delta Electronics (2308 TT): 29 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 30 Sep 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
Delta's board has 7 non-independent and 5 independent directors with a term of three years.
The total number of directors and ratio of independent directors appear higher than most other
listed companies. Besides, according to Delta's CSR report, enhancing the board's functions is
one of the key goals for the company to achieve better corporate governance; also, the board
members' diversity and professionalism will be taken into consideration to ensure a good board
quality. Moreover, the company evaluates all the directors in the board using internal regulations
and an external independent institution each year and at least once every three years,
respectively. We believe Delta has implemented relatively more enhanced measure than most
listed firms to improve its board quality.
Capital management 2
During 2016-19, Delta's payout ratio was stable in the range of c.70%, but fell to 56% in 2020
and 53% in 2021. For 2020, it was mainly due to a sizeable non-cash re-valuation gain after the
acquisition of Delta Thailand (DET), while the company maintained more cash on hand to deal
with the uncertainties caused by COVID-19. However, the DPS growth from 2016 to 2021 was
limited, as Delta struggled with business diversification into new end-markets (eg, smartphone,
NEV). We believe that Delta's effort started to bear fruit in recent years after its NEV business
has gradually picked up. Overall, we see a stable dividend policy from Delta and expect its DPS
to continue to grow YoY in the future given that they declared a TWD11.6 DPS for 2025 which
equals a 50% payout ratio.
Related party &
transaction 1 Revenue from related parties stood at 12% of total 2025 revenue and purchases from relatedparties were at 11%. We see limited risk from related party transactions.
S Supply chainmanagement 1
Delta focuses on: 1) localised management, 2) supplier certification and risk management, and
3) supplier sustainability engagement as key initiatives under the scope of supply chain
management. In 2019, 144 new suppliers joined Delta's supply chain system. All of them passed
environmental and social evaluations, signed the declaration of "non-use of conflict materials",
and complied with Responsible Business Alliance's (BRA) Code of Conduct. Also, these vendors
had to implement and abide by Delta's supply chain CSR policy and code of conduct. To lower
ESG risks, Delta carries out RBA audits and monitors high-risk critical suppliers each year. The
suppliers that fail these audits have to provide detailed improvement plans in response to
identified issues. Besides, Delta has surveyed a total of 263 suppliers with potential risk and 63
suppliers with high risk of the total 1,331 suppliers, based on its CSR policy and code of conduct.
If these suppliers are flagged as high-risk and no concrete improvements are made, Delta may
consider terminating cooperation and transactions with them to improve the company's
engagement on supplier sustainability. Overall, we think Delta has a solid plan to improve its
supply chain management with specific KPIs, including: 1) achieving supplier improvement rate
of 80% in 2025, 2) reducing GHG emissions rate of critical suppliers by 10% in 2025, and 3)
improving water saving rate of critical suppliers by 12% in 2025.
S Data security 2
Delta's Information Security team created Delta's Information Security Policy in November 2013
and started providing educational training to global employees in 2014. Delta gradually
introduced the Information Security Management System (ISMS) in 2016 and obtained the ISO
27001 international certification in 2018. According to ISO27001, Delta has to conduct at least
one internal audit on information security and appoint an independent institution to conduct audit
externally. According to Delta, no major deficiencies have been found in recent years.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

營收走勢圖

研究員/莊佳蓉 EVA8894122@uni-psg.com

研究部對緯穎(6669)維持買進建議，主係：(1) 3Q26營收季增56%，
大幅優於預期；(2) 4Q26在AWS的ASIC氣冷出貨帶動下，預期4Q營
收季增個位數 ；(3) 雖T3水冷機櫃短期遞延， 但預估27年逐月攀升，
公司仍為主要供應商。(4) AMD MI450 於1H27年放量，客戶與平台
更加多元；(5) 新增之Oracle VR200預期的訂單約2.5K，約在27年
貢獻營收。 綜上所述，研究部上修 2026/2027 年營收至1.46兆元
/3.07兆元(原估1.27兆元/2.38兆元)，EPS上修至143.16元/241.16
元，目前評價僅8~9倍，位於低檔，給予12.5倍本益比，目標價3010
元(2027EPS*12.5PER)。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26營收大幅優於預期 
緯穎9月營收為1,726億元，月增19.6%，年增100%，整體3Q26
營收達4,346億元 ，季增56.2%，年增63%，營收達成率達到
135%(研究部預估)/128%(市場共識)， 大幅優於預期 ， 主係Meta
通用型及AWS T3氣冷出貨旺盛所致。3Q26財報尚未公布，研究
部上修至毛利率8.9%(原估8.1%)， 季減0.4pts， 優於原先預期 ，
主係通用伺服器占比較高， 產品組合較佳 ，對毛利率形成支撐，
預估3Q EPS約45.56元。

2. 4Q營運動能可望延續 
展望4Q26，雖水冷機櫃須遞延至年底至1H27才會逐漸放量 ，但
仍預期T3氣冷機櫃及通用持續出貨，預估4Q26營收約4,666億
元，季增7.4%，年增59.5%。毛利率8.2% (原估7.3%)，季減
0.7pts， 主係高單價ASIC水冷機櫃占比尚低， 預估EPS約45.18
元。整體而言，2H26在ASIC氣冷及通用伺服器帶動下，營運明
顯優於上半年

3. T3短期雜音不改長期趨勢，2027年水冷逐步放量 
根據供應鏈調查：(1) T3水冷機櫃初期產線磨合不順，客戶因
此下修今年出貨規劃。不過，2027年水冷機櫃出貨規劃仍逐季
攀升，在晶片端的水冷比重也提升 至50~70%，單價為氣冷3-4
倍以上，成為2027年營收成長的主要動能。在AWS下一代T4產
品，公司持續參與機櫃設計，有望採用全水冷設計，預期28年
出貨，為中長期成長動能。

Document 183: 9月工业企业利润数据点评：采矿业为利润主要拉动，企业库存持续累积

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月工业企业利润数据点评：采矿业为利润主要拉动，企业库存持续累积.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

[Table_RightTitle] 
宏观研究|宏观跟踪报告

[Table_Title] 
采矿业为利润主要拉动， 企业库存持续累积

[Table_ReportType] 
——9 月工业企业利润数据点评

[Table_ReportDate] 2026 年 10 月 27 日

[T able_Summary] ⚫ 企业利润增速持续放缓，营收增速上行。9 月规上工业企业利
润累计同比下跌 0.2 个百分点至 15.5%，当月同比增速由上月
的4.2%上行至14.4%。从不同类型企业来看，国企、股份制企
业利润修复上行，私企、外企利润放缓，企业杠杆率整体小幅
抬升，主要是外企和私企拉动。 
⚫ 营收利润率同比增长，工业品价格延续上行，工业生产增速季
节性回温。PPI 同比由3.8%上行至5%，输入性通胀压力持续，
地缘政治扰动下，PPI 预期延续高位。工业增加值同比由5.2%
上行至5.5%，工业生产季节性上行， 新旧经济生产分化格局边
际收敛。营业收入利润率累计值由5.66%上行至5.68%， 同比上
涨 0.42 个百分点。工业企业营业收入累计同比持平于6.6%，
生产回暖和价格上行带来一定拉动。 
⚫ 企业成本小幅回落，应收账款扩张压力持续。每百元资产实现的
营业收入上行，企业资产使用效率持续改善。每百元营业收入
中的成本回落，费用端保持稳定。营收好转带动企业周转提速，
但库存水平仍高。应收账款同比持续走高，应收账款平均回收
期环比回落，同比上行，资金周转效率依然偏低。 
⚫ 采矿业利润高位攀升，下游高端制造业利润放缓。煤炭、石油和
天然气开采为采矿业主要拉动项，非金属矿采选业拖累作用较
大。地产对上游黑色金属、非金属矿行业拖累明显。化工产业
链受制于价格变动和需求放缓，增速回落。有色金属在价格高
位回落和高基数效应影响下，利润增速高位持续放缓。中游设
备制造表现分化，出口强劲带来支撑。下游制造业中，基数走
高和价格支撑减弱影响下，计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造
业增速高位回落，仍为主要拉动项。销售走弱和行业价格竞争
激烈挤压了汽车的利润空间。运输设备制造业利润回落，主要
受订单交付节奏和基数效应扰动。消费品制造业表现分化。 
⚫ 价格和结构因素带动行业补库， 下游需求和价格仍将制约利润
表现。 工业企业产成品库存持续累积， 但累库速度趋势性放缓。
价格抬升和部分行业补库驱动库存上行，电子、化学原料等行
业在价格、需求等因素下库存累积。内需修复偏慢给营收带来
约束，上、下游企业价格分化的格局仍将持续。四季度财政资
金逐步落地，有望推动制造业和基建投资增长。未来仍需关注
AI 产业链需求放缓和行业利润高基数等带来的影响。 地缘政治
扰动短期难以消退，上游能源产品行业价格预期维持高位。  
⚫ 风险因素：地缘政治风险发展超预期，政策推进和落地不及预
期，地产下滑幅度超预期。

万联证券研究所 www.wlzq.cn 第 3 页 共 7 页  
事件 
2026年1-9月，全国规模以上工业企业实现利润总额6.03万亿元，累计同比较上月下
跌0.2个百分点至15.5%，9月当月同比增速由上月的4.2%上行至14.4%。

1    企业利润当月同比反弹，工业品价格保持增长 
企业利润当月同比反弹上行， 营收增速高位持平， 营收利润率同比保持上涨。2026年
9月， 全国规模以上工业企业利润累计同比由8月的15.7%继续回落至15.5%， 当月同比
增速为14.4%，前值为4.2%。规模以上企业营业收入累计同比持平于上月的6.6%，营
业收入利润率累计值由上月的5.66%上行至5.68%， 环比季节性上行， 同比上涨0.42个
百分点。 
工业品价格延续上行， 工业生产增速季节性回温。9月，PPI同比由3.8%上行至5%，累
计同比由2%继续上行至2.3%， 原油价格上涨带来的输入性通胀压力持续， 上游原料加
工企业利润相对占优， 下游企业利润承压， 行业利润分化格局持续。地缘政治局势依
然紧张，能化产业链价格预期维持高位震荡，PPI或将延续高位。工业增加值同比由
5.2%上行至5.5%，工业生产季节性上行，新旧经济生产分化格局边际收敛。

图表1：工业企业利润当月同比增速大幅反弹（%） 图表2：拆分看，工业品价格保持上行（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

企业营收持续修复， 成本小幅回落， 应收账款扩张压力持续， 费用端保持稳定。9月，
企业每百元资产实现的营业收入由72.4元上行至73.1元，企业资产使用效率持续改
善。每百元营业收入中的成本回落0.05元至85.02元，资源品价格高位给企业成本端
带来的压力持续。企业每百元营业收入中的费用持平于上期的8.35元。 产成品周转天
数由21.3天回落为21天， 企业周转边际提速， 但库存水平仍高。 规模以上工业企业应
收账款同比由8月的9%上行至10%，企业应收账款平均回收期环比由72.2天下行为71.6
天， 较去年同期的69.2天上行， 企业资金周转效率依然偏低， 并且企业应收账款规模
扩张的压力依然较大。

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

国企、 股份制企业利润修复上行，私企利润持续放缓， 外企利利润延续承压，企业杠
杆率整体小幅抬升。2026年9月，分不同企业类型来看，国有企业利润累计同比增速
由上月的10.3%反弹上行至13.1%，上行幅度相对最大。股份制企业利润累计同比由
20.4%小幅上行至20.8%。私营企业由10.4%回落至8.7%，外资企业利润累计同比增速
由2.3%下滑至0.5%，利润依然相对表现较弱。工业企业资产负债率由58.5%小幅上行
至58.6%，国企、股份制企业资产负债率与上月持平，外企和私企资产负债率均小幅
抬升。

图表5：国企利润上行，外企、私企利润回落（%） 图表6：私企、外企资产负债率上行(%)

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

2    采矿业仍为主要支撑，制造业下游行业利润放缓 
采矿业利润增速高位继续上行，制造业利润增速持续回落， 电热气生产和供应业跌幅
进一步加深。三大工业行业来看， 采矿业利润累计同比增速由35.1%继续上行至38.2%，
上行斜率变陡， 为利润主要支撑项， 产成品周转天数小幅增长。 制造业利润总额累计
同比由17.4%进一步回落至17.0 % ， 产成品周转 天数 较上月回落 。 电力、 热力、 燃气及
水的生产和供应业增速由-12%下跌至-14.3%，基数走高带来一定影响，产成品周转天
数与上月持平。

万联证券研究所 www.wlzq.cn 第 5 页 共 7 页  
煤炭、 石油和天然气开采为采矿业主要拉动项， 非金属矿采选业拖累作用较大。从采
矿业内部行业利润表现分化，国际油价上行推动下， 石油、 天然气和煤炭等能源价格
走高， 石油和天然气开采业利润增速持续上行。煤炭开采和洗选业利润累计同比增速
上行5.4个百分点至57%， 上行再度提速， 煤炭开采行业内部供需格局持续改善，冬季
补库需求提升， 价格和需求上行带动行业利润高增。有色金属矿采选业增速高位持续
回落，有色金属价格整体承压， 叠加成本上升挤压行业利润。黑色金属矿采选业增速
今年3月以来持续回落，非金属矿采选业增速跌幅加深，且回落幅度较大，下游地产
开工偏弱，给上游行业带来拖累。

图表7：采矿业利润增速高位继续攀升  （%） 图表8：采矿业中黑色、非金属矿持续下滑（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

地产对上游黑色金属、 非金属矿行业拖累明显， 化工和有色金属行业利润受制于价格
变动。制造业上游细分行业表现分化，能化产业链来看，9月原油价格再度攀升，中
东地区局势紧张， 油价仍将维持高位震荡，化学纤维制造业高位大幅下滑，需求放缓
和高基数效应导致利润增速下行，纺织服装、 服饰业增速回落； 化学原料及化学制品
制造业增速持续回落。 黑色金属冶炼及压延加工业跌幅进一步加深， 非金属矿物制品
增速小幅下行，地产链持续低迷对行业拖累显著。 有色金属冶炼及压延加工业利润同
比高位持续放缓，有色金属价格高位回落叠加高基数效应为主要影响。 
下游制造业增速放缓，中游设备、 消费品制造业利润分化。 中游设备制造业中，电气
机械及器材制造业、 通用设备制造业利润增速放缓， 专用设备制造业增速回升， 出口
强劲给中游制造业形成支撑。下游制造业利润整体回落， 计算机、 通信和其他电子设
备制造业增速高位边际放缓， 基数走高和价格支撑减弱带来一定影响，AI高速发展下
行业景气度预期仍高；铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备制造业增速由10.9%回
落至10%，主要受订单交付节奏和基数效应扰动；汽车制造业由-16%下跌至-22.8%，
销售走弱和行业价格竞争激烈挤压了利润空间。 消费品制造业表现分化，酒、 饮料和
精制茶制造业利润上行幅度相对最大，受低基数、 季节性及去库影响，烟草制品业利
润持续修复，农副食品加工、家具制造、医药利润增速小幅上行，食品制造、造纸、
文娱用品制造业利润增速回落。

3    企业库存持续累积 
价格和结构因素带动行业补库， 下游需求和价格仍将制约利润表现。工业企业产成品
存货累计同比由11.0%继续上行至11.6%， 库存持续累积， 但累库速度趋势性放缓。价
格抬升和部分行业补库驱动库存增速上行， 电子、 化学原料等行业在价格和需求等因
素下库存累积。工业企业营业收入累计同比持平于6.6%，生产回暖和价格上行带来一
定拉动。内需修复偏慢给营收增速带来约束， 上、 下游企业价格分化的格局仍将持续。
四季度财政资金逐步落地， 有望推动制造业和基建投资增长。 未来仍需关注AI产业链
需求放缓和行业利润高基数等带来的影响。 地缘政治扰动短期难以消退， 上游能源产
品行业价格预期维持高位。 
图表11：工业企业库存持续上行（%） 图表12：美国、日本政策不确定性指数小幅上行

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

地缘政治风险发展超预期，政策推进和落地不及预期，地产下滑幅度超预期。

[Table_InudstryRankInfo] 行业投资评级 
强于大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘涨幅10％以上； 
同步大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘涨幅10％至-10％之间； 
弱于大市：未来6个月内行业指数相对大盘跌幅10％以上。

[Table_StockRankInfo] 公司投资评级 
买入：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅15％以上； 
增持：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅5％至15％； 
观望：未来6个月内公司相对大盘涨幅-5％至5％； 
卖出：未来6个月内公司相对大盘跌幅5％以上。 
基准指数 ：沪深300指数

[Table_RiskInfo] 风险提示 
我们在此提醒您， 不同证券研究机构采用不同的评级术语及评级标准。 我们采用的是相对评级体系， 表示投资的
相对比重建议；投资者买入或者卖出证券的决定取决于个人的实际情况，比如当前的持仓结构以及其他需要考
虑的因素。投资者应阅读整篇报告，以获取比较完整的观点与信息，不应仅仅依靠投资评级来推断结论。

出具本报告。 本报告清晰准确地反映了本人的研究观点。 本人不曾因， 不因， 也将不会因本报告中的具体推荐意

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적 
2026-10-29

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 640,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 285,500 원 
상승여력: 124.2%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 감소 및 환율 하락으로 시장 예상치 하회 
Key: 4Q26~1Q27부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 수주 모멘텀 전망 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 엔진&FDC으로 밸류 매력도 충분. 매수 기회

3Q26 Review: 시장예상치 하회하는 실적 시현  
3Q26 연결 매출액 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영업이익 8,992억원
(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(9,792억원)를 하회했다. 조업일수 감소
(휴가 10일+중대재해 4일) 및 환율 하락 영향(450억원)과 더불어, 임단협 관련 예상 비
용 중 75%를 이번 분기 선반영 했기 때문이다. 상선 수주 빈티지는 중형 선박 기준 ‘23년 
9.6%, ‘24년 65%, ‘25년 25%, 대형 선박 기준 ‘22년 4%, ‘23년 45%, ‘24년 31%, 
‘25년 20%로, 선가 상승효과 지속되는 중이다. 특히 해양 부문에서 루야 프로젝트의 공정
률 확대 효과 및 C/O로 180억원의 환입 발생으로 전 분기 대비 큰 폭의 이익 성장 시현했
다. 최근 키윗과 미국 내 합작 조선소 설립을 추진하며, 미 군수지원함 초도 2척 수주 및 향
후 물량 건조, 그리고 HII와의 함정 선체블록 하도급 사업 수주까지 기대할 수 있다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 64 만원으로 하향 
HD현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 82만원에서 64만원
으로 하향한다. 최근 상선 실적 피크아웃 우려에 따른 조선주 주가 급락으로 멀티플 하향 조
정했다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 
FDC에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지속되고 있는 중이다. 올해 공시 기
준으로 육상발전 엔진 약 1.6GW 수주한 것으로 알려졌으나, 미공시된 약 1.4GW까지 포
함 시 총 3.0GW 이상을 수주한 상황이다. 캐파 증설 기준으로 ‘28 년 1.5GW, ‘29 년 
3GW 캐파에 해당하는 물량을 이미 대부분 채웠으며, 기존 계획 대비 빠른 준공 혹은 공정
률 확대, 더 나아가 추가적인 캐파 증설까지 고려할 수준으로 파악된다. 빠르면 올해 연말 
혹은 내년 초부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 본격 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 
전망이다. 현 주가 레벨에서 상승여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 3Q 실적 및 임단협 불
확성 해소된 현 시점에서 적극적 매수 의견 및 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)로 제시한다.

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years. 1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.

4 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.
S&P 500 sub-sectors and industry groups Global Industry Classification Standard (GICS®)/S&P 500 Total Return Index, including Information Technology Total Return (TR) USD;

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

MACRO STRATEGY
The U.S. Capex Boom No One is Talking About—And Its Implications
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
We have long been of the belief that the U.S. is on the cusp of an inbound FDI capex boom
that can result in numerous positive spillover effects for the U.S. economy. The latest

figures from the U.S. Department of Commerce bear this out.
Exhibit 1 highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be inside the U.S.,
with FDI totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year. That is a staggering
103% jump from a year ago and places FDI inflows on track for a record year.
The latest inbound capex already adds to the massive scale of foreign investment in the
U.S. To wit, the accumulated stock of FDI in the U.S. stands at nearly $6T, based on the

latest figures from the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA). Manufacturing accounts for
roughly 43% of the total, and, at last count, some 10,000 foreign affiliates were operating
in the U.S.
These firms are largely invisible to the average U.S. investor, but the activities of U.S.
affiliates of foreign multinationals are substantial and competitive- and growth-enhancing.
To this point and based on the latest data from the BEA, foreign affiliates in the U.S.

employed nearly 8.6 million U.S. workers in 2024. That represents 6.1% of total private
sector employment, with manufacturing and retail the two most prominent sectors
of employment. Roughly 24% of all manufacturing jobs in the U.S. are from foreign
subsidiaries. In terms of pay, U.S. workers at these firms make $89,000 annually, 7% higher
than the national average, according to the Global Business Alliance.
Foreign affiliates also contributed some $1.5T to U.S. GDP; comprised some 15.7% of total

U.S. private sector investment; accounted for 12.4% of total U.S. research & development
(R&D) expenditures; paid roughly 20% of all U.S. federal taxes; and made up 22.5% of
U.S. exports and 26.2% of U.S. imports. 1 These are the direct effects of U.S. subsidiaries
of foreign companies operating in the U.S. The indirect effects are even greater in terms
of more income, jobs, growth and tax revenue—all bolstering the long-term competitive
advantage of the U.S. In a nutshell, foreign firms operating in the U.S. are an important

ingredient of U.S. economic growth.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Companies that once served the U.S. market exclusively through exports
increasingly face higher costs and more uncertainties and hence want to be “inside.”
Two, with a $32T-plus economy with extraordinary consumer purchasing power, foreign

firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment than just trade (exports). There
is no more dynamic consumer in the world than the American shopper.
Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers, semiconductors, power
generation, etc., has created numerous investment opportunities for many foreign firms
that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging capex cycle.
Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly developed

technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.
As more U.S. affiliates of foreign multinationals set up shop in the U.S., think more job
creation across the country, more manufacturing output, more locally sourced purchases,
more U.S. exports, more spending on R&D, and more tax revenue for local communities.
And over the long run, think a more globally competitive U.S. economy.
1 Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign Multinational Enterprises, 2024” and “Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign

Multinational Enterprises, 2023”, BEA July 2026.
Investment Implications
The boom in foreign investment in
the U.S. should continue to support
employment, productivity, corporate
earnings, and broader U.S. economic
competitiveness, in our view. Hence
our U.S. bias.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
And speaking of global competitiveness, Exhibit 2A is very illustrative of the magnet pull of
the U.S. economy to attract FDI versus the rest of the world. Of new greenfield investment
this decade, note the overriding preference for the U.S., accounting for nearly 16% of total
global greenfield investment this decade. America’s share is larger than the next three
countries combined—the United Kingdom, India, and Germany. And note that America’s

share of new investment is more than 5X that of China’s. Most of this new investment
has gone into manufacturing sectors like electrical equipment, computers, appliances and
batteries and has originated from Asia and Europe.
The bulk of foreign investment comes from Europe, followed by Japan and Canada, but as
Exhibit 2B highlights, there is a new dynamic playing out when it comes to America’s ability
to attract capital. There is a new source in town: a handful of developing nations (namely

Taiwan, South Korea, Mexico and Brazil). Mexican manufacturers, Taiwan technology
leaders, South Korea chip leaders, Brazil industrial firms, India pharmaceutical firms—these
companies and more are realizing that in today’s trade environment, the best way to serve
the American market is not necessarily through exports but through investment—investing
locally. FDI flows from developing nations reached $63B in the 12 months through June,
more than double the level a year prior ($24.5B), according to the BEA. Fueled by insatiable

AI demand, flows from South Korea alone totaled $23.4B in the first half of 2026. The
upshot is that an increasingly important source of capital for the U.S. is coming from an
unlikely source: the developing nations.
More fire for the capex boom. The coming boom in FDI inflows is supportive of our thesis
that the U.S. stands on the cusp of a major industrial upgrade. We are in the early innings
of a structural—not cyclical—capex cycle that should lead to the rebuilding of domestic

supply chains, higher value-added manufacturing, and more AI-driven productivity growth.
Foreign firms, in our opinion, will be part of this process.
That is important to recognize because remember the following: when a foreign company
builds a factory or an R&D facility or expands its existing manufacturing capacity right
here in the U.S., good things happen. Demand for U.S. goods and services goes up (as does
demand for construction, materials, logistics and professional services). New jobs are created,

boosting incomes of U.S. workers, while supporting more local spending and consumption.
And because many foreign firms often bring advanced manufacturing technologies when
investing in the U.S., the end results include a boost in productivity and innovation in various
sectors of the economy.
All the above, of course, are the secret ingredients to stronger U.S. economic growth, rising
corporate earnings and higher equity prices. With a little help from our friends, America’s

industrial renewal is gaining strength and momentum.

MARKET VIEW
Europe’s Electoral Cycle and Market Risks
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
As midterm elections approach for the U.S. next month, voters across Europe will also
enter their own election season starting this November and continuing throughout
2027. Over the next year, 52% of the total EU population, representing 46% of EU GDP
across eight countries, will go to the polls, including voters in three of its four largest

economies (Exhibit 3). Having announced snap general elections earlier this month,
Spain will vote for members of its lower and upper house on November 29. A series of
parliamentary elections are then due for 2027 in Estonia (by March 7), Finland (on April
18), Greece (by July 25), Slovakia (by September 28), Poland (by November 11) and Italy
(by December 22). And perhaps most consequentially France will stage Presidential
elections on April 18, with a potential second round of voting set for May 2.

For the EU as a whole, the significance of these coming elections will be the potential
for a shift toward more populist, domestically oriented policy priorities from leading
opposition candidates (particularly in France, Spain and Poland) which could obstruct the
process of closer EU integration. In the nearer term, adoption of the new seven-year
(2028-2034) EU budget due by the end of 2027 could be impeded. And over the longer
term, renewed efforts toward an EU-wide capital market union to deepen and attract

more inward investment into European markets could also be further delayed.
At the country level, investors have seen bond yields rise globally in 2026 on the back of
higher inflation and concerns over fiscal sustainability. The debt question has arguably
been most pressing in parts of Europe, especially given the likelihood that upcoming
elections make for domestic increases in government largesse. And of the eight
countries going to the polls over the next year, France remains the biggest source of

concern. Interest rate spreads on French 10-year sovereign bonds have overtaken those
in both Greece and Italy this year (Exhibit 4), driven by investor concerns over public
debt. The 2027 government budget unveiled earlier this month projected a deficit of
5.0% of GDP (well in excess of the 3% specified in the EU Stability and Growth Pact),
but remained insufficiently expansive to nullify the risk that the current government
could be unseated later this year if it is forced through the fragmented parliament. This

comes on top of an existing debt load of close to 120% of GDP and a halving of official
2026 growth forecasts from 1.0% at the start of the year to 0.5% in September. In
short, all four variables in France’s debt sustainability equation currently stand as major
headwinds, and the two leading candidates in the 2027 election have shown limited
appetite for fiscal consolidation over the coming years.

Investment Implications
We remain cautious on European
markets overall, despite seeing
pockets of strength in selected
areas such as defense, data center
infrastructure and renewable energy.
On top of growing uncertainty
from the upcoming electoral cycle

and interest rate outlook, rising
competition and slowing demand
from China continue to challenge
key segments of the Consumer
Discretionary sector. And limited
direct AI concentration also leaves
Europe under-exposed to the key
growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and
emerging Asia.

Intervention from the ECB under its 2022 bond purchase program is also unlikely at this

stage given that the spread widening in France has not appeared either disorderly or
unwarranted. Meanwhile, conditions could potentially be aggravated by expected ECB rate
hikes in response to still above-target inflation across the eurozone. Indeed recent moves
to tighten monetary policy from a number of major central banks have been read most
hawkishly in Europe. Most of the increase in European bond yields has come from higher
inflation breakevens as opposed to real yields in the U.S. and Japan. And despite some

paring back of tightening expectations in the wake of recent bond volatility, the market is
still pricing three more policy rate increases over the next 12 months.
Euro depreciation and an emerging shift toward more protectionist trade policy with China
could further complicate the ECB policy outlook. China posted a record $35B trade surplus,
according to Eurostat, with the EU in September (alongside its $938B total surplus for the
first three quarters of 2026), and the two markets have agreed to reduce the EU bilateral

deficit by capping imports of hybrid cars for four years. Hybrids account for less than 2%
of total EU imports from China, but this could potentially mark the start of a broader shift
toward more trade protection in Europe with its largest foreign supplier which risks adding
further upward pressure to cost-push inflation. And though the leading candidates in the
French election may favor resuming oil and gas imports from Russia, Europe also remains

among the most at risk from the global energy shock. Indeed some 56% of the region’s
crude oil consumption is in the form of diesel product according to the International Energy
Agency, more than twice the U.S. share.
The 2- to 10-year eurozone yield curve has already flattened significantly since the start
of the year. And at just over 30 basis points as of mid-October, it remains flatter than
its equivalents in the U.S., UK and Japan. Outside Energy and Technology, Financials have

been among the strongest-performing sectors in Europe this year. But additional ECB
rate hikes could lead to further curve flattening, compressing bank net interest margins
and contributing to slower growth—particularly given that rate increases are coming in
response to supply-driven inflation rather than reflecting stronger economic activity.
We nonetheless continue to see pockets of strength in European markets. Growing
defense budgets should remain a tailwind for Industrials as national governments look to

meet their North Atlantic Treaty Organization targets over the next several years. And the
sector should be further supported by indirect exposure to AI investment through electric
cabling, power infrastructure and building materials for data centers. Leaders in renewable
energy equipment and services are also likely to be beneficiaries of grid upgrades and
broadening electrification globally, particularly given elevated fossil fuel prices. But our view

on the region remains cautious overall. On top of growing uncertainty from the upcoming
electoral cycle and interest rate outlook, rising competition and slowing demand from
China continue to challenge key segments of Europe’s Consumer Discretionary sector.
Limited direct AI concentration in technology hardware, cloud services and application
software also leaves Europe underexposed to the key growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and emerging Asia.

6 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Cautious Optimism Entering Fiscal Year 2027
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
“A renewed focus on rebuilding fiscal space is urgent, given elevated debt, higher
borrowing costs, multiplying demands on public budgets and heightened uncertainty.” So
warned the IMF about the global fiscal outlook last week in its updated World Economic

Outlook report. Much is at stake for developed nations in Europe and Asia facing high
existing debt, unfavorable demographics and more modest growth.
For the U.S., between structurally higher mandatory outlays, the administration’s $1.5T
proposed defense package, and the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield sitting above 5%, spending
is likely headed higher. We continue to fade concerns of an impending U.S. debt crisis,
however, largely because economic growth continues to surprise to the upside. Expanding

output is good news for the debt-to-GDP ratio, which remains a touch below 100%.
Indeed, rather than being led higher on account of un-anchored inflation expectations, the
10-year U.S. Treasury yield has repriced to reflect better-than-expected nominal growth
(Exhibit 5A). Higher yields in the face of slowing growth, meanwhile, would be a different
story, not only for the fiscal outlook (worse) but also for U.S. equity valuations (lower).
Of course, after the U.S. accumulated another $2T deficit in FY 2026, 2 “rebuilding fiscal

space” in the IMF’s words will be politically unpopular and mathematically challenging.
In our view, the biggest near-term tail risk would be a surprise move lower in growth as
budget deficits at 6% of GDP in good times leave little room for bad times. This isn’t our
base case given robust consumer spending per our internal credit and debit card data and
ongoing investment in the AI buildout and beyond. We expect these two pillars to sustain
growth in 2027.

The other good news is that, contrary to concerns, demand for Treasurys remains robust.
Yields are attractive—that was the message from October’s successful $39B 10-year
Treasury note auction. At the same time, U.S. debt boasts an array of buyers: domestic
households, the Federal Reserve, mutual funds and exchange-traded funds, money market
funds, pension funds, foreign central banks, private investors overseas, and more. Per
Exhibit 5B, whereas foreign official investors once dominated some 40% of the U.S.

Treasury market during the 2008/2009 Great Financial Crisis, their share has fallen to
12% while domestic private investors now account for a majority of holdings. Translated:
demand is more diverse than most appreciate.

In our view, higher nominal and real
yields present a compelling entry point
for investors seeking income and can
provide cushion in the face of higher
rate volatility. We expect demand for
U.S. assets to remain healthy and favor
the U.S. over developed economies in
Europe facing more modest growth and
fiscal instability.

Document 280: Cliff Notes_ the source and persistence of inflation (2026-11-01)

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
11:16 October 30 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI. Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in
the month, largely owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in domestic and
international holiday travel & accommodation. The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than

anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).

### Page 2

While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation – housing in particular looks to be cooling faster
than expected as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are still showing persistence.
Highlighting this is market services ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator of price
pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail
points to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for another chunky read in Q4.

With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts, and some of the energy risks flagged
then materialising, we continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp rate hike to
4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part
of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding
instead to wait for further information before taking the next step with policy. The decision was made

4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A 25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening
in 2027 (Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems probable; however, each
decision will depend on fuel prices, inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady at its October meeting. Based on prior comments by key
members, the decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook and Cleveland Fed

President Hammack’s dissents in favour of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in
September). Chair Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic growth outside of
tech-related investment (evinced by Q3 GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in the statement. However, inflation “remains

elevated” and the FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable return of annual inflation
to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target. This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will help
the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests another 25bp hike is likely in December.
Thereafter, the FOMC will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related risks through
2027; although, in the absence of another shock, we expect them to remain on hold.

The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view on the breadth of growth, the 3.4%
annualised gain for GDP benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household consumption,
12.7% growth in non-residential investment as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment was effectively offset by net exports’ drag
given most of the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is imported. This is not to say that

US firms won’t benefit from the investment once complete, but simply that much of the initial return
goes to the foreign businesses who provide the necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks remain for the consumer, the increase
in nominal spending seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis, consumption flat in the
month following a 0.5% gain in August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the outlook is

### Page 3

Related articles
expected to see household consumption growth average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr
medium-term target as the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline and core
measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy rates unchanged in October. Of note from the
meeting communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks for inflation, but also downside

risks for growth. "While the full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying inflation has
remained broadly stable so far and wages do not show a material response". "Financing conditions
have [also] tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth". In the press conference,
President Lagarde noted that, between the October and December meetings, a significant amount of
new economic information will be released, allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.

Based on the October meeting communications, we continue to expect a rate hike at the December
meeting but believe this will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the spectrum of risks
Europe faces is broad and skewed towards inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that she will step down as ECB President in
February ahead of the scheduled conclusion of her term next October.

WEEKLY WEBCAST: Possible
Bad News & Good Buying
Opportunities Ahead
By Ed Yardeni • 28 Oct 2026
View in browser

When we lowered S&P 500 year-end target a month ago, it had nothing to
do with the market’s fundamental underpinnings. Our concerns remain
valuation related in light of rising uncertainties in the investment backdrop.
But don’t be tricked by scary surprises that send stocks southward; they
could be attractive buying opportunities, say Ed and Elias, given the
robust economic growth and corporate earnings we continue to expect.
Today, they explain why prospective frights from four directions—

### Page 2

geopolitical, US political, the Fed, and AI—could turn out to be
opportunities in disguise.

Document 502: ブラジル大統領選、ルラ氏が逆転勝利も政権運営には難路 ～議会での右派勢力拡大が足かせに、財政健全化と家計支援の両立が焦点～

Source PDF: all report pdf/ブラジル大統領選、ルラ氏が逆転勝利も政権運営には難路 ～議会での右派勢力拡大が足かせに、財政健全化と家計支援の両立が焦点～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

World Trends

2026 年10 月 26 日

1/4 
ブラジル大統領選、ルラ氏が逆転勝利も政権運営には難路 
～議会での右派勢力拡大が足かせに、財政健全化と家計支援の両立が焦点～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ブラジルでは、10 月 25 日に実施された大統領選挙の決選投票において、左派で現職のルラ
大統領が右派のフラビオ・ボルソナロ氏を破り、再選を決めた。第１回投票ではフラビオ氏
が首位となったものの、ルラ氏は選挙戦略の立て直しや支持者の動員強化などを通じて劣勢
を挽回し、民主化以降で初となる決選投票での逆転勝利を収めた。ただし、投票時間の延長
を巡る混乱などを背景に、選挙後の政治的対立が長期化する懸念も残る。 
○ 一方、同時に実施された議会選挙では、右派ＰＬが上下院で最大勢力となる見通しであり、
ルラ氏の政策運営は困難に直面することが予想される。経済政策では、最低賃金の引き上げ
や低所得者支援などを通じて個人消費を下支えする方針が続くとみられるものの、政府債務
が高水準にあるなか、財源確保と財政健全化の両立が課題となる。金融市場においては、フ
ラビオ氏による財政改革への期待が後退することで、短期的に株安・レアル安が進む可能性
がある一方、その後の動向については経済チームの人事や具体的な財政措置、議会との合意
形成に左右されると考えられる。 
○ 外交面では、中国やＢＲＩＣＳ諸国との連携を重視する姿勢が続く一方、米国との通商・投
資面での実務的な調整が不可欠となる。中南米で右派政権が広がるなか、ルラ氏の再選は域
内の左派勢力にとって重要な意味を持つものの、次期政権の安定性は、議会との協調関係の
構築や財政運営への信認確保を実現できるかに左右されることになろう。

【現職のルラ氏が決選投票で第１回投票での劣勢を挽回し、通算４期目の政権発足へ】 
ブラジルでは、10 月 25 日に実施された大統領選挙の決選投票において、左派ＰＴ（労働者党）
から出馬した現職のルラ大統領が、右派ＰＬ（自由党）のフラビオ・ボルソナロ上院議員を破り、
再選を決めた。ブラジル高等選挙裁判所（ＴＳＥ）は開票率97.69％の段階でルラ氏の当選を発表
するとともに、最終的なルラ氏の得票率は51.79％、 フラビオ氏は48.21％となった。 これにより、
ルラ氏は2027 年１月５日から通算４期目の政権を担うことになった。 
10 月４日に実施された第１回投票では、フラビオ氏が47.03％、ルラ氏が45.16％を獲得し、事

### Page 2

2/4 
前予想を覆してフラビオ氏が首位で決選投票に進んだ（注１）。投票前日に公表された世論調査に
おいても、フラビオ氏が 51％、ルラ氏が 49％となっていた。ただし、同調査の誤差は上下２ポイ
ントとされており、選挙直前まで両者の争いは拮抗していた。今回の結果は、ルラ氏が第１回投票
での劣勢を挽回したことを示しており、 民主化以降の大統領選において第１回投票で２位となった
候補が決選投票で逆転勝利するのは初めてとなった。 
ルラ陣営は、 第１回投票後に選挙戦略を立て直し、 インターネットを通じた発信や支持者の動員
を強化してきた。有権者の間では、ボルソナロ一家の政権復帰を警戒する動きも広がりをみせるな
ど、こうした取り組みが支持の積み増しにつながった可能性がある。事実、棄権率は第１回投票の
21.08％から、決選投票においては19.21％に低下している。とはいえ、決選投票においてルラ氏が
逆転劇を演じることになった要因を判断するには、 最終的な地域別得票や投票率の変化を見極める
必要がある。 
投票当日には公共交通機関の運行を巡る問題や大雨の影響などを受けて、 投票時間の延長が決定
された。最高裁判所（ＳＴＦ）は投票時間を現地時間の25 日 19 時まで延長する決定を下したもの
の、ＴＳＥは 17 時時点で投票を終了していなかった投票所に限って延長を認め、集計・結果公表
は 19 時以降に再開された。こうした対応を巡って混乱も生じており、選挙手続きに対する異議申
立てにつながる可能性も指摘されている。 選挙結果を巡る政治的な対立が長期化すれば、 政策協議
の遅れのほか、金融市場の不安定化につながる可能性も考えられる。

【議会選では右派が勢力を拡大しており、ルラ氏にとって再選後の政策運営は困難に直面】 
ルラ氏の再選が確定した場合でも、 政権運営を取り巻く環境には厳しさが残る。大統領選の第１
回投票と同時に実施された 10 月４日の議会選挙では、フラビオ氏の所属するＰＬが下院で121 議
席を獲得し、ＰＴの70 議席を上回った。上院においてもＰＬは19 議席を獲得し、非改選分と合わ
せて 29 議席となる見通しで、ＰＬは上下院双方で最大勢力となることが見込まれる。 
このため、ルラ氏が大統領職を維持しても、社会政策や財政措置を実現するには、中道政党を含
む議会勢力との調整が欠かせない。 政策ごとに協力を取り付ける余地はあるものの、 その過程にお
いて予算配分などを巡る要求が強まれば、 財政健全化との両立のハードルは高まる。次期政権の政
策実行力を評価するうえでは、 大統領選の勝敗に加え、 議会との協力関係をどこまで構築できるか
が焦点となる。

【家計支援の継続が見込まれる一方、財源確保と債務安定化が課題となる】 
経済政策では、ルラ氏が掲げる最低賃金の実質的な引き上げ のほか、生活必需品に対する減税、
低所得者に対する税還付、 零細事業者向け支援などを踏まえると、 再選後も家計の購買力を支える
政策が重視されると予想される。 こうした政策は、 近年の同国経済成長のけん引役となってきた個

注１ 10 月５日付レポート「ブラジル大統領選は決選投票へ、フラビオ氏が首位」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666284.html）

### Page 3

3/4 
人消費の下支えにつながることが期待される。その一方、実施方法によっては、歳出増や歳入減に
よる財政への悪影響が懸念されることから、安定的な財源を確保することが必要になる。 
2023 年にルラ氏が大統領に就任して以降、社会支出の拡充などを背景に財政運営への懸念がくす
ぶっている。利払い負担の増大や行政訴
訟による判決債務（プレカトリオス）の
増大 など による 歳出増 を受けて 公的 債
務残高の対ＧＤＰ比は上昇しており、
2026 年８月末時点では 82.9％となって
いる（図１） 。債務残高が増加するなかで
ルラ氏が公約に掲げる 家計支援を拡充
するには、歳出の優先順位を見直し、中
期的な財政運営への信認を維持するこ
とが不可欠となる。財政に対する懸念を
理由に長期金利が上昇基調を強めれば、
政府の利払い負担が増大し、政策に充てられる財源は一段と制約されることも予想される。 
現地報道によれば、ルラ政権の経済チームは、歳出抑制を強める憲法改正案のほか、政策融資の
見直しを検討していると報じられている。しかし、正式な提案の内容については、ルラ氏自身の判
断のほか、次期政権の経済チームの構成に左右される。今後は、公約の実現に必要な財源と債務安
定化への道筋とともに、それを議会との合意につなげられるかが注目される。

【金融市場では選挙前の期待の修正が起こる一方、その後は具体的な政策が評価を左右するか】 
選挙前の金融市場においては、フラビ
オ氏による財政改革への期待がブラジ
ル資産を支える動きが確認された。第１
回投票が行われた直後には、主要株価指
数のボベスパ指数とともに、通貨レアル
の対ドル相場も大幅に上昇するなど、株
高・レアル高が進んだ（注２）。一方で、
フラビオ氏の公約は、大規模な歳出削減
を掲げる一方、社会給付の維持なども盛
り込まれていたため、具体策の実現性に
市場の期待が 先行する面もあった と考
えられる。選挙戦終盤にかけては、 ルラ氏がフラビオ氏を追い上げるとの見方が広がるなか、 株価 ・
